Well, I wouldn't necessarily say sad, exactly. I really don't want to sound like a complainer, because I know I have a good life. But... I guess for a long time now, there's just been this sort of... gray, flat feeling most days. If that makes sense?

Well, um... I wouldn't say it's anything too drastic. I really do try to take good care of myself, I promise I'm not neglecting my health or anything like that.

Did that answer what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Well... gosh, I'm trying to think of something. I'd really like to give you a positive answer, I really would. I don't want to sit here sounding like a... a wet blanket. \n\nBut, um... if I'm being completely honest, nothing is really jumping out at me right now. I mean, I still get my work done, and I like making sure the systems are running smoothly for my team—that's satisfying, I suppose. But as for actual, genuine pleasure? I just... I haven't really felt that spark with anything lately. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably a frustrating answer and I really don't mean to be difficult. Am I being helpful at all with these answers?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now feel a lack of interest or pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm sorry, I feel like I might not have answered you very well just a moment ago, since we're sort of circling back to this. I really don't have a specific list of things I've stopped doing or completely lost interest in, if that's what you mean. \n\nI try my absolute best to keep up with everything I've always done. I'd hate to be the kind of guy who just gives up on his hobbies or lets things fall by the wayside. So I still do the yard work, and I still go out to the events my wife and I like... it's just that, like I mentioned, the feeling I get from them is a bit more muted now. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm trying to be as clear as I can for you, I just don't want you to think I'm sitting around doing nothing. Am I giving you what you need?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's anything too drastic. I really do try to take good care of myself, I promise I'm not neglecting my health or anything like that. \n\nBut, I suppose my appetite has been a little off lately... more days than not, if I'm being perfectly honest. I'll get caught up with the systems at work and just sort of work right through lunch without even noticing I missed it. And then by dinner, I'm really just picking at my plate. My wife actually pointed out recently that my pants are looking a bit looser, which surprised me since I'm definitely not trying to lose any weight. \n\nI hope that's not too concerning? I'm just trying to give you the full picture... is that the kind of information you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your movement or activity level, such as feeling restless or slowed down?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh, no... I don't think I've experienced anything like that. I really do try my best to stay active and keep moving, I promise. \n\nI wouldn't say I'm restless or pacing around, and I definitely don't feel like I'm moving in slow motion or anything. I'm still walking around the office, checking on the servers and my team just like always. I really don't want you to think I'm just dragging my feet or letting myself go physically, because I'm honestly doing okay in that department. I feel like I'm moving at my normal pace. \n\nDoes that sound okay to you? I just want to make sure I'm answering these questions the right way for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I wouldn't say anything like that. I'm so sorry if I gave you the impression that things were that bad. \n\nI mean, sure, I've been feeling a bit down, but I'm definitely not having thoughts of... well, of not being here. I have a good life, a wonderful wife, a steady job managing the IT department... I really have a lot to be thankful for, and I try to focus on that. I would never want to do anything to hurt my family or just, you know, give up. I'm really trying my best to push through this slump. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm in some sort of crisis? I just want to reassure you that I'm perfectly safe. Is that what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the gray, flat feeling and lack of pleasure in activities?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been a little while now. I really hope it doesn't sound like I've just been letting this drag on without trying to fix it, because I really have been trying to stay positive. \n\nBut if I had to put a timeline on it... I suppose it started right around the time they pulled me back into full operational leadership at work. That was, oh, about two years and four months ago, roughly. It's just been a lot of responsibility, and I guess this sort of gray feeling just crept up on me since then. I've really been doing my best to handle it all, though, I promise I'm not just wallowing in it. \n\nIs two years a terribly long time to be feeling like this? I hope you don't think I'm a lost cause or anything for waiting to get help.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before, where you felt a gray, flat feeling and lacked pleasure in activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I don't think so. I really don't want you to think this is some lifelong issue or that I've always been, you know, a downer. I've always tried to be a steady, reliable person for my team and my wife. \n\nI mean, everyone has their off days, right? But nothing that lasted this long or felt quite this... heavy. Before they moved me back into this leadership role, I was doing really well, I promise. I was enjoying my hobbies and work was fine. So, no, this is really the first time it's stuck around like this. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right history so you don't think there's something fundamentally broken with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant improvement in your mood or interest in activities during the past two years?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not really, no. I mean, I've tried, I really have. I don't want you to think I haven't been making an effort to snap out of it. I try to put on a good face for my wife, maybe plan a nice dinner or something, but... no, the gray feeling just sort of stays there. \n\nThere hasn't been a time where I woke up and felt like my old self again, if that's what you mean. Is it normal for it to just... not lift at all? I really hope I'm not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced two weeks or more where you felt this gray, flat feeling and lacked pleasure in activities?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, definitely. Does that make sense, based on what I told you before? I'm really trying to be as accurate as I can for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods like this in your life before the past two years?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, really, I haven't. At work, at home... I've never had any sort of history of, um, emotional issues or anything like that. I really hope you believe me. I don't want to come across as someone who's always been struggling or who can't handle things. \n\nBefore this promotion, everything was perfectly fine. I was completely myself. Is that... is that the kind of background information you're looking for?
